- issue-010 (high)
  - issue: InventoryAggregate.available_qty is a material high-severity temporal/semantic defect: it exposes a nightly inventory snapshot as unqualified current availability and allocation rules use it for intraday allocation as if it were current allocatable stock.
  - target: InventoryAggregate.available_qty names and uses a nightly snapshot as if it were current allocatable inventory. Source finding context: InventoryAggregate.available_qty and inventory allocation rule Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:86-90,111-112 Source finding context: `available_qty`가 매일 밤 저장되는 스냅샷인데 일중 주문 할당의 가용 재고로 쓰여 시간 의미가 이름과 맞지 않는다. Source finding context: `available_qty`라는 이름은 할당 시점의 가용 수량을 뜻하지만, 정의상 값은 야간 스냅샷이다. 시간 기준(`as_of`, snapshot time, reservation cutoff)이 없어서 '현재 가용'과 '스냅샷 가용'이 같은 개념으로 합쳐진다. Source finding context: 속성명을 `available_qty_snapshot`처럼 시간성을 드러내거나, `as_of_at`, 집계 기준, 예약/할당 반영 범위를 함께 모델링한다. 일중 할당은 실시간 ATP/예약 원장 등 별도 권위 개념을 참조하게 한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-002 Source finding context: 주문·재고 개념의 시간성과 운영 위험을 통합 기준 문서 안에서 명확히 하는 목적. Source finding context: 일중 주문/예약/출고로 재고가 변한 뒤에도 allocation rule이 전일 야간 스냅샷 `available_qty`를 현재 가용 재고로 사용할 때. Source finding context: 가용 재고 의미가 과거 스냅샷인지 현재 할당 가능량인지 불명확해져 과판매, 미할당, 시스템 간 재고 드리프트 위험을 만든다. Source finding context: 시간 기준이 있는 스냅샷 값을 시간 기준 없는 현재 가용 재고 이름으로 노출했다. Source finding context: `available_qty`는 '가용 수량'으로 정의되어 현재 할당 가능한 수량처럼 보인다. Source finding context: 동일 속성 설명은 이 값이 매일 밤 배치로 저장되는 스냅샷이라고 한다. Source finding context: 재고 할당 규칙은 일중 주문도 이 스냅샷 값을 읽는다고 하여 현재 가용 의미와 스냅샷 의미의 불일치를 운영 경로에 연결한다.
  - failure condition: After intraday orders, reservations, or shipments change stock, allocation rules still read prior nightly snapshot available_qty as current availability. Source finding context: 일중 주문/예약/출고로 재고가 변한 뒤에도 allocation rule이 전일 야간 스냅샷 `available_qty`를 현재 가용 재고로 사용할 때.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Fix the model before release by renaming or remodeling available_qty as a time-qualified snapshot, including as_of_at and the reservation or allocation basis used to compute it. Intraday allocation should depend on a separate real-time ATP or reservation-ledger authority so current allocation decisions are not made from a prior nightly snapshot.
  - remediation: Fix the model before release by renaming or remodeling available_qty as a time-qualified snapshot, including as_of_at and the reservation or allocation basis used to compute it. Intraday allocation should depend on a separate real-time ATP or reservation-ledger authority so current allocation decisions are not made from a prior nightly snapshot.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-004 (medium)
  - issue: The ontology materially lacks source-authority and override/audit evidence for operational values that are reconciled across systems or manually changed, especially inventory quantity and shipment ETA.
  - target: The ontology lacks audit evidence and authority concepts for values that are reconciled across systems or manually overridden. Source finding context: Manual adjustment and multi-source authority evidence coverage Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:43-45; logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:76-85; logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:110-112 Source finding context: The ontology lacks audit evidence and single-authority concepts for values managed across systems or manually adjusted. Source finding context: Coverage for WMS/TMS/ERP integration must include who can override operational facts, when, why, and which source wins. Without those concepts, the model cannot audit inventory reconciliation or ETA overrides, and downstream systems cannot distinguish carrier-reported facts from human-edited operational estimates. Source finding context: Add SourceAuthority/SourceSystem ownership for shared values and an Adjustment or OverrideEvent concept with actor_ref, occurred_at, reason_code, source_value, adjusted_value, approved_by, and affected field. Use it for ETA and inventory reconciliation at minimum. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-004 Source finding context: Authority, auditability, and operational risk control for WMS/TMS/ERP integration. Source finding context: When WMS and ERP quantities disagree, or when an ETA is manually changed after carrier data arrives, the ontology cannot identify the authoritative value or preserve evidence of the intervention. Source finding context: The concept baseline leaves high-risk operational controls outside the model, weakening dispute resolution, SLA explanations, and reconciliation audit trails. Source finding context: Shared and manually adjusted operational values are represented only as final scalar attributes, without authority or adjustment-event concepts. Source finding context: InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand notes that WMS and ERP each hold values and are reconciled nightly to ERP, but the model has no authority or reconciliation event entity. Source finding context: Shipment.eta is a scalar datetime manually adjusted by operations, and the notes say carrier API values and manual adjustments are mixed into a final operations-judgment value.
  - failure condition: When WMS and ERP quantities disagree, or when an ETA is manually changed after carrier data arrives, the ontology cannot identify the authoritative value or preserve evidence of the intervention.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Add SourceAuthority or SourceSystem ownership for shared operational values and add an Adjustment or OverrideEvent concept covering actor_ref, occurred_at, reason_code, source_value, adjusted_value, approved_by, and affected field. Apply this at minimum to ETA overrides and inventory reconciliation so authority and audit evidence are modeled before the ontology proceeds as an integration governance baseline.
  - remediation: Add SourceAuthority or SourceSystem ownership for shared operational values and add an Adjustment or OverrideEvent concept covering actor_ref, occurred_at, reason_code, source_value, adjusted_value, approved_by, and affected field. Apply this at minimum to ETA overrides and inventory reconciliation so authority and audit evidence are modeled before the ontology proceeds as an integration governance baseline.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-005 (medium)
  - issue: Order, Shipment, and Tracking lifecycle coverage is materially incomplete because it models the normal success path but not common exception, reverse-logistics, and post-terminal fulfillment paths.
  - target: Order, Shipment, and Tracking lifecycle models omit common terminal and exception paths needed for fulfillment operations. Source finding context: Order, Shipment, and Tracking lifecycle coverage Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:14-17; logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:39-42; logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:55-63; logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:106-108 Source finding context: Coverage is incomplete for operational lifecycle states after or instead of successful delivery. Exceptions and post-delivery corrections are normal in fulfillment, and independent OMS/TMS/carrier status streams need a concept that can absorb them without making delivered the only terminal state. Source finding context: Extend lifecycle coverage with explicit terminal and exception states, plus lifecycle event concepts for cancellation, backorder, return/RMA, delivery failure, loss/damage, correction/reissue, and closure. Define how carrier TrackingEvent.exception maps into shipment/order lifecycle transitions. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-005 Source finding context: Status model completeness for order, shipment, and delivery operations. Source finding context: When a shipment fails, is returned, corrected after delivery, or a carrier reports an exception, the ontology has no first-class lifecycle path except successful delivery or independent unmapped tracking events. Source finding context: The integration baseline cannot consistently represent exception handling, reverse logistics, or post-terminal correction, making status reconciliation fragile across OMS, TMS, and carriers. Source finding context: Lifecycle coverage is success-path oriented and leaves exception/post-delivery states outside the core Order and Shipment status models. Source finding context: Order.fulfillment_status includes received, allocated, picking, shipped, delivered only. Source finding context: Shipment.status includes created, picked, in_transit, delivered only and states that delivered is final. Source finding context: TrackingEvent includes exception, but carrier events and internal statuses are managed independently and mapped by each integration.
  - failure condition: When a shipment fails, is returned, corrected after delivery, or a carrier reports an exception, the ontology has no first-class lifecycle path except successful delivery or independent unmapped tracking events.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Extend lifecycle coverage before the ontology advances as a fulfillment operations baseline. Add explicit terminal and exception states and lifecycle event concepts for cancellation, backorder, return/RMA, delivery failure, loss/damage, correction/reissue, and closure, and define how carrier TrackingEvent.exception drives shipment and order lifecycle transitions.
  - remediation: Extend lifecycle coverage before the ontology advances as a fulfillment operations baseline. Add explicit terminal and exception states and lifecycle event concepts for cancellation, backorder, return/RMA, delivery failure, loss/damage, correction/reissue, and closure, and define how carrier TrackingEvent.exception drives shipment and order lifecycle transitions.

- issue-008 (medium)
  - issue: DeliveryLeg inherits Shipment.status.delivered as its final state, so an intermediate route leg is modeled as if completing that leg were the same terminal event as delivering the whole shipment to the customer. This is a retained medium issue, scoped as a downstream manifestation of the broader DeliveryLeg-as-Shipment modeling problem.
  - target: DeliveryLeg inherits a shipment-level final-state rule that over-constrains intermediate route legs. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / Shipment.status and DeliveryLeg inheritance Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input, `Shipment.definition`: "배송 구간(DeliveryLeg)도 Shipment의 일종이다."; `Shipment.attributes.status.values`: `[created, picked, in_transit, delivered]`; `Shipment.attributes.status.note`: "delivered가 최종 상태다."; `DeliveryLeg.is_a: Shipment`; `DeliveryLeg.definition`: "화물 경로의 한 구간(예: 창고→허브, 허브→고객)." Source finding context: `DeliveryLeg` inherits a shipment-level final-state rule that is satisfiable for a whole shipment but logically over-constrains intermediate route legs. Source finding context: The conflict is between the necessary inheritance claim (`DeliveryLeg is_a Shipment`) and the necessary status-finality claim attached to `Shipment`. Under the explicit example `창고→허브`, the leg's completion condition is arrival at a hub, but the inherited final status is `delivered`; this forces the same entity type to use one terminal state for both leg-complete and shipment/customer-delivered. That weakens the document as a WMS/TMS/ERP concept baseline because downstream integrations cannot consistently decide whether `delivered` closes a route segment or the customer shipment. Source finding context: Separate `DeliveryLeg` from `Shipment` inheritance, or split the state model into `Shipment.status` and `DeliveryLeg.status` with distinct terminal meanings such as `arrived_destination` for a leg and `delivered_to_customer` for a shipment. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/logic.findings.yaml#logic-candidate-001 Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP integration concept baseline for shipment and delivery concepts, especially status model consistency. Source finding context: A multi-leg route includes an intermediate leg such as warehouse-to-hub and uses inherited `Shipment.status.delivered` as the leg's terminal status. Source finding context: The same terminal status can represent two different closure events, so integrations may close the whole shipment when only a leg has completed, or fail to close a leg without implying customer delivery. Source finding context: The ontology models route segments as subclasses of whole physical shipments while placing an unqualified final delivery state on the parent type. Source finding context: `DeliveryLeg` is declared as a subtype of `Shipment`. Source finding context: All `DeliveryLeg` instances inherit the `Shipment.status` enum and its unqualified final `delivered` rule. Source finding context: The inherited final state must also apply to intermediate route legs such as warehouse-to-hub legs.
  - failure condition: A multi-leg route includes an intermediate leg such as warehouse-to-hub and uses inherited Shipment.status.delivered as the leg's terminal status. Source finding context: A multi-leg route includes an intermediate leg such as warehouse-to-hub and uses inherited `Shipment.status.delivered` as the leg's terminal status.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Fix the DeliveryLeg redesign before relying on status semantics in integrations. Either remove DeliveryLeg from Shipment inheritance and model it as a segment/composition of a shipment, or split the status model so Shipment.status and DeliveryLeg.status have distinct terminal meanings, such as delivered_to_customer for shipments and arrived_destination for legs. The ordering matters because the final-state vocabulary cannot be made reliable while the parent inheritance still imposes Shipment's unqualified delivered state on every leg.
  - remediation: Fix the DeliveryLeg redesign before relying on status semantics in integrations. Either remove DeliveryLeg from Shipment inheritance and model it as a segment/composition of a shipment, or split the status model so Shipment.status and DeliveryLeg.status have distinct terminal meanings, such as delivered_to_customer for shipments and arrived_destination for legs. The ordering matters because the final-state vocabulary cannot be made reliable while the parent inheritance still imposes Shipment's unqualified delivered state on every leg.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing
- issue-011 (medium)
  - issue: The ontology must not treat the shared label `delivered` as a single semantic state across order fulfillment, shipment status, and carrier tracking events. Each use has a different authority and completion condition, so the current model leaves delivery completion meaning ambiguous.
  - target: The label delivered appears in order, shipment, and tracking status models with different authorities and completion conditions but no ontology-owned disambiguation. Source finding context: Order.fulfillment_status, Shipment.status, TrackingEvent.event_type Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:19-22,44-47,63-66,111-113 Source finding context: `delivered`가 주문 상태, 화물 상태, 캐리어 이벤트에서 같은 표기로 쓰이지만 각 시스템이 독립 관리한다고 되어 있어 동음이의 의미가 구분되지 않는다. Source finding context: 주문이 delivered라는 것은 주문의 모든 필요한 fulfillment가 완료되었다는 OMS 판단일 수 있고, shipment delivered는 특정 화물의 도착 완료, tracking delivered는 캐리어가 보고한 이벤트 발생을 뜻한다. 같은 문자열이 서로 다른 권위와 완료 조건을 갖는데, 문서는 매핑을 각 연동에 맡겨 의미 기준을 제공하지 않는다. Source finding context: 상태 값을 네임스페이스 또는 별도 개념으로 구분하고, `carrier_delivered_event`, `shipment_delivery_completed`, `order_fulfillment_delivered`처럼 권위와 완료 조건을 명시한다. 세 모델 간 전이/매핑 규칙은 통합 기준 문서 안에서 표준화한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-004 Source finding context: 주문·화물·배송 상태 모델의 의미 정합성과 시스템 간 precedence를 정의하는 목적. Source finding context: OMS, TMS, 캐리어 이벤트 중 하나의 `delivered`를 다른 시스템의 완료 상태와 동일시해 주문 완료, 고객 알림, 정산, SLA 판단을 수행할 때. Source finding context: 동일 표기 상태가 서로 다른 권위와 완료 조건을 갖기 때문에 배송 완료의 기준 시점과 책임 시스템이 통합마다 달라진다. Source finding context: 서로 다른 시스템 상태의 동일 표기 값을 의미적으로 네임스페이스화하거나 매핑하지 않았다. Source finding context: Order.fulfillment_status는 OMS 관리 주문 단위 진행 상태이며 `delivered` 값을 포함한다. Source finding context: Shipment.status는 화물 상태이며 `delivered`를 최종 상태로 둔다. Source finding context: TrackingEvent.event_type도 캐리어 보고 이벤트로 `delivered`를 포함하고, Shipment.status와의 매핑은 각 연동에 맡긴다. Source finding context: Integrity rule은 세 상태가 각 시스템에서 독립 관리된다고 하므로 동일 표기 값의 의미 동일성을 보장하지 않는다.
  - failure condition: An OMS, TMS, or carrier delivered value is treated as equivalent to another system's delivered state for order completion, customer notification, settlement, or SLA decisions. Source finding context: OMS, TMS, 캐리어 이벤트 중 하나의 `delivered`를 다른 시스템의 완료 상태와 동일시해 주문 완료, 고객 알림, 정산, SLA 판단을 수행할 때.
  - candidates: fix_before_release, fix_now
  - rationale: Namespace or separate the status concepts by authority and completion condition, such as carrier delivered event, shipment delivery completed, and order fulfillment delivered. Then define standard transition and mapping rules among carrier events, shipment completion, and order fulfillment completion before downstream integrations rely on status equivalence.

Then define standard transition and mapping rules among carrier events, shipment completion, and order fulfillment completion before downstream integrations rely on status equivalence.

  - issue: Shipment.total_weight is a material medium issue because it is a bare numeric derived total even though SKU weights and dimensions may arrive in different source units. It should be retained as a distinct shipment aggregate correctness issue, while recognizing it as a narrower manifestation of the broader measurement-unit problem in issue-003.
  - target: SKU weight and dimension units may vary by source, but Shipment.total_weight stores only a bare numeric total. Source finding context: Sku.weight, Sku.dims, Shipment.total_weight Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/execution-preparation/materialized-input.md:33-38,40-49 Source finding context: SKU 중량·치수의 단위가 소스별로 다른데 Shipment.total_weight는 합계로 저장되어 단위 의미가 계산 결과에 보존되지 않는다. Source finding context: 중량 합계는 같은 측정 단위의 값끼리만 의미가 있다. SKU 단위가 kg/lb로 섞일 수 있는데 total_weight에 단위나 변환 기준이 없으면 이름이 가리키는 '총중량'과 실제 저장값의 의미가 불일치한다. Source finding context: 중량과 치수를 값+단위 구조로 모델링한다. `weight_value`, `weight_unit`, `dimension_unit` 또는 표준 단위 환산 규칙을 두고, `total_weight`도 단위와 계산 기준 시점을 함께 저장한다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/semantics.findings.yaml#semantics-candidate-005 Source finding context: 화물·재고 단위 개념의 정합성을 통합 기준 문서로 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: Shipment.total_weight는 구성 OrderLine 중량의 합으로 계산해 저장된다. Source finding context: 단위가 다른 숫자들이 합산될 수 있으므로 `total_weight`라는 이름의 총중량 의미가 실제 계산값과 어긋난다.
  - failure condition: SKU weights from different warehouses or source systems with different units are combined into one Shipment.total_weight calculation. Source finding context: 서로 다른 창고나 소스 시스템의 SKU 중량이 하나의 Shipment.total_weight 계산에 섞일 때.

This should be handled alongside the broader measurement modeling work for issue-003, because fixing the shipment total depends on a shared representation for compatible units and conversions.

Source finding context: This weakens structural completeness for integration because OMS order lines and TMS/WMS shipment execution cannot be reconciled through the model; downstream systems would need out-of-model joins or assumptions. Source finding context: The fulfillment relation is modeled only at Shipment-to-Order granularity while OrderLine has no outgoing or incoming fulfillment relation. Source finding context: OrderLine contains sku_ref and qty but no Shipment, DeliveryLeg, or fulfillment-allocation reference. Source finding context: The ontology's only declared relation connects Shipment to Order rather than Shipment to OrderLine. Source finding context: The relation note explicitly excludes OrderLine-level mapping.

- issue-015 (medium)
  - issue: DeliveryLeg is structurally under-modeled as a route segment: it has sequence and endpoint fields, but no parent Shipment or route composition path that says which end-to-end shipment it belongs to.
  - target: DeliveryLeg instances lack a structural parent path to the shipment route they compose. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / DeliveryLeg as Shipment subtype Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: Shipment definition says DeliveryLeg is also a kind of Shipment; DeliveryLeg is_a Shipment and has leg_seq/from_ref/to_ref; no attribute or relation links DeliveryLeg instances to a parent end-to-end Shipment. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg lacks a structural parent path to the shipment route it composes. Source finding context: The target intends to model shipment and delivery, and DeliveryLeg is defined as a route segment. A sequence number alone is not structurally meaningful without a parent route/shipment scope. Because DeliveryLeg inherits Shipment rather than being linked as a component, the model cannot represent which legs belong to the same shipment or reconstruct a route as connected segments. Source finding context: Model DeliveryLeg as a component of Shipment with a required shipment_ref or relation such as Shipment has_leg DeliveryLeg, keeping leg_seq scoped to that parent shipment. If legs remain subtypes, add a parent_shipment_ref for route composition. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-003 Source finding context: Concept baseline for shipment and delivery path integration. Source finding context: When a Shipment has multiple route segments, the ontology cannot determine which DeliveryLegs compose that shipment or whether their sequence belongs to the same route. Source finding context: This breaks structural route completeness for TMS-style shipment execution and tracking because segment data cannot be reliably connected into one shipment movement. Source finding context: The model uses inheritance for DeliveryLeg but omits a composition relation from Shipment to DeliveryLeg. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg is defined as a route segment with leg_seq/from_ref/to_ref. Source finding context: DeliveryLeg is declared as is_a Shipment, making it a Shipment subtype rather than an explicit component connected to a parent Shipment. Source finding context: No relation or attribute connects DeliveryLeg instances to a parent end-to-end Shipment.
  - failure condition: When a Shipment has multiple route segments, the ontology cannot determine which DeliveryLegs compose that shipment or whether their sequence belongs to the same route.
  - candidates: follow_up
  - rationale: Model DeliveryLeg as a component of Shipment by adding a required parent shipment_ref or an explicit Shipment has_leg DeliveryLeg relation, and scope leg_seq to that parent shipment. If the subtype modeling is retained for compatibility, add an explicit parent_shipment_ref so route composition is still represented.
  - remediation: Model DeliveryLeg as a component of Shipment by adding a required parent shipment_ref or an explicit Shipment has_leg DeliveryLeg relation, and scope leg_seq to that parent shipment. If the subtype modeling is retained for compatibility, add an explicit parent_shipment_ref so route composition is still represented.
  - verification: verify the remediation against the failure condition with a focused check before closing

### Recommendations
- issue-003 (high): Weight, dimension, order quantity, and inventory quantity values lack explicit unit and conversion semantics for cross-system integration. Source finding context: Measurement unit coverage for SKU and shipment quantities Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:28-33; logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml:35-44 Source finding context: Weight and dimension values lack explicit unit-of-measure coverage despite cross-system unit variation. Source finding context: Freight rating, carrier selection, packing, customs-adjacent data, and warehouse handling depend on comparable units and dimensional weight. A WMS/TMS/ERP concept standard that allows raw numeric weights and string dimensions from mixed unit systems cannot provide a stable integration contract. Source finding context: Add structured measurement concepts: weight_value, weight_uom, dimension components, dimension_uom, measurement_source, measured_at/effective_at, and conversion rules. Define Shipment.total_weight as derived from normalized line or package measurements with a declared unit. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/coverage.findings.yaml#coverage-candidate-003 Source finding context: Freight and shipment consistency across warehouse, transport, and ERP integrations. Source finding context: When different warehouses or source systems provide kg/lb or dimension strings with different units, shipment totals and carrier-facing data cannot be compared or safely aggregated. Source finding context: The ontology can produce ambiguous or incorrect freight weights, dimensions, and downstream transportation decisions despite claiming to be an integration baseline. Source finding context: Measurement values are modeled as bare numbers or free-form strings rather than unit-qualified measurements. Source finding context: Sku.weight is a number and the note says warehouses use kg or lb according to local practice. Source finding context: Sku.dims is a string and the note says units differ by source system. Source finding context: Shipment.total_weight stores the sum of OrderLine weights without declaring unit or conversion basis. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml: quantity, weight, and dimensions attributes Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: `Sku.weight` note says warehouse-specific measuring conventions, kg or lb, are used as-is; `Sku.dims` note says dimensions are a string and units differ by source system; `OrderLine.qty`, `InventoryRecord.quantity_on_hand`, and `InventoryAggregate.available_qty` are plain numbers without unit-of-measure attributes. Source finding context: 단위 모델이 값 속성에 붙어 있지 않아 창고·국가·ERP 기준이 추가될수록 기존 숫자 필드의 의미 연속성이 깨진다. Source finding context: 확장 가능한 통합 온톨로지라면 값과 단위를 분리해 새 단위계를 추가해도 기존 데이터를 재해석할 수 있어야 한다. 현재 구조에서는 단위 권위가 각 소스 시스템 관례에 묻혀 있어, 새 시스템 추가가 필드 추가나 전역 변환 규칙 수정으로 이어진다. 이는 ERP 회계 수량, WMS 물리 수량, TMS 중량 계산 간 통합 충돌을 만든다. Source finding context: `Measurement`/`Quantity` 값 객체를 도입해 `value`, `unit`, `uom_authority`, `measured_at` 또는 `basis`를 명시한다. `dims`는 구조화된 length/width/height와 단위로 분해하고, 주문/재고 수량에는 판매 단위·보관 단위·환산 규칙을 별도 개념으로 둔다. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/evolution.findings.yaml#evolution-candidate-003 Source finding context: WMS/TMS/ERP 통합의 단위 기준과 재고·화물 계산의 확장 가능성을 제공하는 목적. Source finding context: kg/lb 창고 혼재, 해외 거점 추가, SKU별 포장 단위/판매 단위 차이, ERP와 WMS의 단위 기준 차이를 통합해야 하는 조건. Source finding context: 단위 없는 숫자와 단위가 섞인 문자열은 새 소스가 추가될 때 기존 값의 의미를 보존하지 못하고, 중량 합산·재고 가용성·운임 계산 같은 핵심 통합 계산을 재구성하게 만든다. Source finding context: 측정값을 단위와 기준을 가진 독립 개념으로 모델링하지 않고 원천 시스템 관례에 의존했다. Source finding context: `Sku.weight` stores a number while the note allows either kg or lb depending on warehouse measuring convention. Source finding context: `Sku.dims` stores dimensions as a source-dependent string rather than structured dimensions with units. Source finding context: Order and inventory quantities are plain numbers with no explicit unit-of-measure or conversion basis.
- issue-016 (low): TrackingEvent is not structurally connectable to a specific delivery leg. Source finding context: logistics-fulfillment-ontology.yaml / TrackingEvent linkage Source finding context: Embedded Materialized Input: TrackingEvent has shipment_ref to Shipment; DeliveryLeg is_a Shipment; no relation assigns events to a specific DeliveryLeg or leg sequence. Source finding context: For a route with multiple DeliveryLegs, a carrier event such as arrived_hub or exception needs a structural attachment point to a leg or stop to preserve delivery-path context. The current single shipment_ref can point at the broad Shipment concept while the route segments are not composed under it, so event-to-leg connectivity is underspecified. Source finding context: Add an optional or required leg_ref from TrackingEvent to DeliveryLeg, or define event scoping rules plus a Shipment-to-DeliveryLeg composition relation that lets events be associated with a leg by route location and sequence. Source finding context: .onto/review/20260716-e3fc23c8/round1/structure.findings.yaml#structure-candidate-004
